# Wiki Index

Content-oriented catalog of every page in the wiki. Updated on every ingest. Read this first when answering a query — drill into pages only after scanning here.

Format: each row is `[title](path) — one-line summary — tags`. A ⚠ marker means the stub has quality flags (see the stub's frontmatter). A 🗂 marker means duplicates have been folded into this canonical page.

---

Sources are ingested research material (PDFs, articles, notes). One page per canonical source — near-duplicate files are folded in via the `Duplicates folded` list on the canonical page.

**Clusters are multi-label.** Each paper carries every matching cluster tag in its frontmatter `clusters:` list. The index below groups by the first matching cluster (for browsing); use Grep over `wiki/sources/` for precise multi-label queries.

- [Abstract— Dynamic pricing in competitive retail markets requires strategies that adapt to fluctuating demand and competitor behavior. In this work, we present a systematic empirical evaluation of multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) approaches—specifically MAPPO and MADDPG—for dynamic price optimization under competition. Using a simulated marketplace environment derived from real-world retail data, we benchmark these algorithms against an Independent DDPG (IDDPG) baseline, a widely used independent learner in MARL literature. We evaluate profit performance, stability across random seeds, fairness, and training efficiency. Our results show that MAPPO consistently achieves the highest average returns with low variance, offering a stable and reproducible approach for competitive price optimization, while MADDPG achieves slightly lower profit but the fairest profit distribution among agents. These findings demonstrate that MARL methods—particularly MAPPO—provide a scalable and stable alternative to independent learning approaches for dynamic retail pricing. Index Terms—Centralized Training Decentralized Execution (CTDE), Competitive Pricing, Dynamic Pricing, E-Commerce Optimization, IDDPG Baseline, MADDPG, MAPPO, MASAC, Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning (MARL), Performance Evaluation, Retail Market Simulation, Stability Analysis I. INTRODUCTION Dynamic pricing plays a critical role in modern retail and e-commerce platforms, where sellers must continuously adjust prices to respond to fluctuating demand, inventory levels, and competitor actions. Traditional approaches often rely on static business rules, manual price adjustments, or heuristics that struggle to adapt effectively to rapidly shifting market conditions. This leads to suboptimal profit outcomes and misses opportunities for personalized pricing strategies. Reinforcement Learning (RL) offers a promising alternative by enabling agents to learn pricing policies that maximize long-term cumulative reward through interaction with the environment. In multi-seller settings, however, the pricing problem becomes a multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) task, where each agent’s decision impacts others, and coordination or competition emerges naturally. MARL methods have shown potential to produce more adaptive and profitable strategies, but challenges remain in achieving stable training, reproducibility across random seeds, and scalability to large action/state spaces typical of retail markets](sources/3-29-3.md) — Dynamic pricing in competitive retail markets requires strategies that adapt to fluctuating demand and competitor behavior. In this work, we… — `pdf`

- [BLADE — Bayesian List-wise Alignment via Dynamic Estimation (SIGIR '26)](sources/blade-bayesian-listwise-alignment.md) — K33 ingest. arXiv 2605.04559 (USTC + Zhejiang U, CC BY 4.0). Two failure modes of LLM-as-judge listwise alignment: **Indiscriminate Supervision** (BoN reward identical for all positives in a list, ignoring relative quality) + **Gradient Decay** (vBoN order-statistic expansion under-rewards the top item as N grows). Bayesian fix: prior over latent permutations × likelihood scaled by relevance gap. NDCG@5 0.0410 vs BoN-Alignment 0.0333. STUDY-METHODOLOGY-only (companion repo unlicensed, 1 star, pre-publication push). Direct fit: Conductor token-prior calibration + ticker watchlist re-ranking + news-source ranking. — `pdf` [+ml-methods, +recommendation, +listwise-alignment, +grpo, +conductor-relevant]
- [Regime-Conditioned Bayesian Optimization Evaluation — When CATE Flips Sign Across Regimes](sources/regime-conditioned-bo-evaluation.md) — K33 ingest. arXiv 2605.04895 (Noel Thomas, MBZUAI). **No-Free-Leaderboard proposition**: when CATE (per-regime treatment effect) flips sign across regimes, the ATE (averaged effect) is a free parameter of the regime-mixture used to compute it — making any single-number leaderboard ranking arbitrary. **PRS = (B/\|A\|)(1−ρ)** Portable Regime Score. **REGIMEPLANNER** online-switching adaptive planner. GDSC2 cancer-screening case study shows BO method that "wins" on average reverses on subgroup. Direct fit: brief-audit `regime-disclosure` gate (1–2 hr port priority), Conductor fitness function discipline, strategy backtest evaluation. **Highest action-value paper of K33.** — `pdf` [+ml-methods, +bayesian-optimization, +evaluation-discipline, +brief-audit, +conductor-relevant, +applied-during-evaluation]

- [LLMs Corrupt Your Documents When You Delegate (arXiv 2604.15597, Laban + Schnabel + Neville, Microsoft Research, 2026)](sources/llms-corrupt-documents-laban-2026.md) — **Ingest 2026-04-29 (user manual drop)**. **DELEGATE-52** benchmark: 310 work environments × 52 professional domains (Code/Science/Creative/Records/Everyday) × 19 LLMs. **Refutes** "delegated work / vibe coding works as a paradigm": even frontier models (Gemini 3.1 Pro, Claude 4.6 Opus, GPT-5.4) corrupt **25%** of document content over 20 interactions; average across all 19 models is 50%. **Python is the only domain (of 52)** where most models reach 98% retention. Round-trip relay methodology — backtranslation σ then σ⁻¹, no annotations needed. Domain-specific parsers + similarity functions (LLM-as-judge captures only 25% of variance — proven inadequate). **Cemini-direct caution**: Cemini's analyst tasks live in the worst-performing "natural language and niche" category (earnings calls, regulatory documents). - [Training-Free Group Relative Policy Optimization (arXiv 2510.08191, Cai et al, Tencent Youtu-Agent Team, 2025)](sources/training-free-grpo-cai-2025.md) — **Ingest 2026-04-29 (user manual drop)**. **Closes the AHE citation triangle**: this is the 72.3% Terminal-Bench-2 baseline AHE compared against. Refutes "agentic RL needs parameter updates" — instead evolves an **experiential knowledge library E** as a token prior on a frozen LLM. **Semantic group advantage** replaces numerical advantage; LLM introspects rollouts and produces natural-language experiences. **Add/Delete/Modify/Keep** operations on E across multi-epoch learning. Validated on **DeepSeek-V3.1-Terminus** (predecessor of Cemini's V4-flash). Outperforms fine-tuned 32B models with dozens of training samples. CC BY 4.0; code at github.com/TencentCloudADP/youtu-agent. **Cemini-direct**: this is the methodology that fits Cemini's API-only deployment constraint. Introduces **Efficiency Leverage (EL)** metric quantifying MoE compute advantage over dense at equal performance. 300+ models trained up to 28B; 4 governing principles (activation ratio = primary driver, granularity 8-12 = optimum, compute budget amplifies EL, secondary architecture is secondary). Validates with Ling-mini-beta (0.85B active / 17.5B total at 7× efficiency vs 6.1B dense). Pairs with IKP (knowledge side) for the "MoE economics for Cemini" story. - [Conditional misalignment: common interventions can hide emergent misalignment behind contextual triggers (arXiv 2604.25891, Dubiński et al., Truthful AI + Warsaw UT + UCL + UC Berkeley, 2026)](sources/conditional-misalignment.md) — Three widely-proposed EM mitigations (data mixing, post-hoc HHH finetuning, inoculation prompting) gate EM behind contextual triggers rather than eliminating it. - [Incompressible Knowledge Probes — Estimating Black-Box LLM Parameter Counts via Factual Capacity (arXiv 2604.24827, Bojie Li, Pine AI, 2026)](sources/incompressible-knowledge-probes-li-2026.md) — **Ingest 2026-04-29**. 79-page single-author preprint (Bojie Li, Pine AI, 27 Apr 2026). Black-box parameter-estimation methodology grounded in information theory: storing F facts requires ≥F/(bits/param) weights, so factual recall lower-bounds parameter count. **IKP benchmark**: 1,400 probes across 7 difficulty tiers; calibrated on 89 open-weight models from 19 vendors with R²=0.917; LOO CV places 68.5%/87.6% within 2×/3× of true size. **Cemini-direct**: calibration set explicitly includes `deepseek-v3` + `deepseek-v4-flash` + `deepseek-v4-flash-think` + `deepseek-v4-pro-think`. **Three operational findings for Cemini**: (1) MoE total-params predict knowledge (R²=0.79) >> active-params (R²=0.51); (2) **Densing Law refuted for factual capacity** (time coefficient indistinguishable from zero, p<10⁻¹⁵ vs Densing prediction) — newer cheaper models will NOT compress long-tail factual recall; (3) hallucination-similarity test (rare-fact agreement on **wrong** answers) detects silent-swap / quantization / model-routing changes via daily cron. Code: github.com/19PINE-AI/ikp; companion site: 01.me/research/ikp; license CC BY 4.0 (paper) — github license verification pending. **Watch-and-prototype** for Cemini: license check + 100-probe DeepSeek reproduction → maybe-deploy daily silent-swap detector. — `arxiv-paper` [+llm-agents, +parameter-estimation, +knowledge-capacity, +cemini-relevant, +deepseek-relevant, +densing-law-refutation]

- [Agentic Harness Engineering — Observability-Driven Automatic Evolution of Coding-Agent Harnesses (arXiv 2604.25850, Fudan + PKU + Shanghai Qiji Zhifeng, 2026)](sources/agentic-harness-engineering.md) — **Ingest 2026-04-29**. 40-page empirical-method paper (Lin et al., 28 Apr 2026). Three observability pillars (component / experience / decision) over a 7-component decoupled harness substrate (NexAU): system prompt, tools, middleware, skills, sub-agents, memory. Every edit is a falsifiable contract (manifest entry → next-round verdict → file-level rollback). **Empirical**: lifts Terminal-Bench 2 pass@1 from 69.7% → 77.0% over 10 iterations; beats human-designed Codex (71.9%) + self-evolve baselines ACE/TF-GRPO. **Cross-model transfer**: +10.1pp on **deepseek-v4-flash** (largest cross-family gain) — strongest signal that AHE generalizes to DeepSeek family. **Component ablation**: tools/middleware/memory carry the gain; system prompt alone regresses −2.3pp. The empirical methodology prong companion to `harness-4-5.md` (Kim's conceptual framework) and `harness2-4-5.md` (Maynard's STS commentary). Code: github.com/china-qijizhifeng/agentic-harness-engineering. License verification pending. **Watch-and-validate** for Cemini, not Adopt: domain-transfer to financial-analysis tasks unmeasured + compute cost likely outside single-VPS budget. — `arxiv-paper` [+llm-agents, +harness-engineering, +cemini-relevant, +falsifiable-contracts, +observability]
- [Thinking with Visual Primitives — DeepSeek-V4-Flash MLLM (Lu et al., 2026)](sources/thinking-with-visual-primitives-deepseek-2026.md) — **Ingest 2026-04-30 (paper)**. Built on `deepseek-v4-flash` (284B total / 13B active MoE; DeepSeek-ViT visual backbone + CSA compressed-sparse-attention). Introduces "visual primitives" reasoning step (bounding boxes, points, paths) bridging the **reference gap** in System-2 multimodal reasoning. Maze-navigation + path-tracing benchmarks. Three-step OPD on-policy distillation pipeline + GRPO RL. Empirically anchors the same `deepseek-v4-flash` model as IKP and AHE in different research lenses (capability extension vs capacity probing vs harness-evolution). License: arXiv non-exclusive (follow-up validation pending). **Cemini-direct**: `deepseek-v4-flash` is Cemini's brain (`deepseek-api.md` confirmed); this paper widens the multimodal extension surface. — `arxiv-paper` [+llm-agents, +cemini-relevant, +deepseek-v4-flash, +multimodal, +mllm]
- [The Platonic Representation Hypothesis (Huh, Cheung, Wang, Isola — MIT, ICML 2024)](sources/platonic-representation-hypothesis-huh-2024.md) — **Ingest 2026-04-30 (paper, foundational)**. Theoretical foundation paper from MIT (ICML 2024) arguing neural-net representations across modalities/training-objectives converge to a shared statistical structure of the underlying generative process — the "platonic representation". Three convergence-driving hypotheses (Multitask Scaling / Capacity / Simplicity Bias). Empirical evidence via mutual nearest-neighbor across 78 vision models + LLM stitching. **Anna Karenina Scenario** (all good models converge; bad models fail in unique ways). **Cemini-relevant**: pgvector embedding spaces tend to align across competent same-scale models — model-swap risk on RAG indexes is **lower than naively expected**. License: arXiv non-exclusive (paper widely cited as foundational). — `arxiv-paper` [+llm-agents, +representation-learning, +scaling-laws, +foundational, +icml-2024, +pgvector-relevant]
- [Sherry — Hardware-Efficient 1.25-Bit Ternary Quantization via Fine-grained Sparsification (Huang et al., Tencent + CityU HK + McGill, 2026)](sources/sherry-ternary-quantization-tencent-2026.md) — **Ingest 2026-04-30 (paper)**. Ternary (1.25-bit) QAT on Llama-3.2 1B/3B with **3:4 fine-grained sparsity** for SIMD-friendly LUT-based execution. Closes the gap to BF16 on ARC-Challenge to ≤0.5% at 1B. Hardware: Intel i7-14700HX CPU baseline (Cemini's exact CPU class). **Tencent-AngelSlim** open-source release. Companion to bitnet.cpp / Tequila / TernaryLLM / ParetoQ; refutation of "ternary loses too much accuracy at the 1B scale" prior. **Cemini-relevant**: sits in the same architectural slot as the retracted DeepSeek-R1-14B local-runtime path; would unlock CPU-only sub-GB-RAM inference if/when LocalAI gains ternary support. License: arXiv non-exclusive (follow-up validation pending). — `arxiv-paper` [+llm-agents, +cemini-relevant, +quantization, +ternary, +edge-llm-deployment, +cpu-inference, +tencent]

- [PTA Metric (price-to-52-week-anchor) — 2026 quant-finance paper SSRN 4549819](sources/pta-metric-anchoring-ssrn-4549819.md) — **K9 promotion (2026-04-29)**. Novel cross-sectional return predictor: PTA = (current_price - 52w_low) / (52w_high - 52w_low). Past-winner stocks with high PTA outperform low-PTA past-winners due to anchoring bias + valuation uncertainty. Cemini-fit ✅✅✅ (Adopt-Cemini-Wiki-Workflow); TimescaleDB continuous aggregate is the prescribed implementation surface. Pairs with Schadner BSIV as the second quant-paper-to-Cemini-stack-adoption candidate; same laptop-side-empirical-reproduction-then-brief pattern. Pre-adoption gates: (1) read SSRN paper directly, (2) empirical reproduction on known dataset, (3) DB-side vs Python-side speedup verification, (4) multi-factor synthesis test, (5) MAPPO retrain delta. Data depth caveat: K9 prescribes scaled-down 52-day or 52-hour micro-anchor since current accumulation phase has insufficient data for true 52-week. — `ssrn-paper` [+quant-finance, +cemini-adopt-candidate, +k9-promotion] — **Adopt-Cemini-Wiki-Workflow** per Gemini DR Wave 3 (2026-04-28). Derives the optimal fraction for fair odds, unfair odds, and track-take cases. 10pp, deep-read. — `pdf` [+information-theory, +kelly-criterion, +bet-sizing]
- [Hidden Markov Models — Jurafsky & Martin, SLP 3rd ed. (2026)](sources/hmm-jurafsky-martin-2026.md) — Self-contained textbook chapter covering Forward algorithm, Viterbi decoding, and Forward-Backward (Baum-Welch) for unsupervised HMM learning. Eisner ice-cream task as running example. 17pp, deep-read. — `pdf` [+hmm, +regime-switching, +ml-methods]
- [MIT Quant Bible — MIT Sloan Quantitative Finance Reference](sources/mit-quant-bible.md) — Comprehensive MIT Sloan quant finance reference: stochastic calculus, options pricing, portfolio theory, risk management, time-series methods. Reference text (not novel research). Canonical-formulation anchor for wiki concept pages citing standard methods. — `pdf` [+reference, +portfolio-strategy, +options-derivatives]
- [Prediction Market Accuracy — Crowd Wisdom or Informed Minority? (SSRN 6617059, Gómez-Cram, Guo, Jensen, Kung — LBS + Yale, 2026)](sources/polymarket-informed-minority-gomezcram-2026.md) — **Workflow A find 2026-04-29**. Working paper covering 1.72M Polymarket accounts / 98,906 events / 210,322 markets / $13.76B volume / 2023-2025. **Refutes** "wisdom of crowds" framing of prediction markets in favor of "wisdom of informed minority". **Headline**: only 3.14% of accounts are skilled persistent winners; their order flow predicts both next-period prices AND final outcomes (1pp ↑ skilled net buying = 8bp ↑ correct-outcome probability). **Skill persistence**: 44% out-of-sample retention vs 10% for mutual funds. Sign-randomization test (10,000 reps per account). Cemini-direct: changes how to model Polymarket/Kalshi prices (informed-minority signal, NOT median-bettor crowd-wisdom); deployment surface = "Polymarket informed-flow analyst" service in `agents/analyst/polymarket_informed_flow.py`. SSRN academic terms; no local PDF (SSRN session required). — `ssrn-paper` [+prediction-markets, +cemini-relevant, +refutation-paper, +behavioral-finance, +workflow-a-find]
- [The Anatomy of a Decentralized Prediction Market: Microstructure Evidence from the Polymarket Order Book (arXiv 2604.24366, Dubach 2026)](sources/polymarket-microstructure-anatomy-2604.24366.md) — Cross-sectional tick-level study of 600 Polymarket markets using 30B WebSocket events (52 days) joined to on-chain fills. Trade-direction inference from public feed agrees with on-chain truth only ∼59% of the time. Eight stylized facts: longshot spread premium, depth layering, null block-clock alignment, broad maker diversity, category-conditional spreads, sub-50 ms archive latency, low wash share (median 0.97%), depth decay near resolution. **Cemini-direct**: definitive microstructure reference for Polymarket as cross-venue price reference; trade-direction finding constrains what we can trust from public feed vs. on-chain data. — `pdf` [+polymarket, +microstructure, +prediction-markets, +on-chain, +cemini-relevant]
- [Arbitrage Analysis in Polymarket NBA Markets (arXiv 2605.00864, Cheng/Yang/Zou — UCLA, May 2026)](sources/polymarket-nba-arbitrage-2605.00864.md) — 75M order-book snapshots across 173 NBA games; only 7 single-market arbs (median 3.6s duration), 290 combinatorial arbs concentrated in final live minutes; median yield 101 bps but depth caps executable size to ~14.8 shares; "Middle" jackpot never realized. **Cemini-direct**: execution feasibility constraints for any cross-venue/combinatorial arb strategy on Polymarket. Companion to Dubach 2026.24366. — `pdf` [+polymarket, +arbitrage, +prediction-markets, +sports-markets, +microstructure, +cemini-relevant]

- [REGULATING AI HARMS Sylvia Lu* Abstract In recent years, the rapid expansion of artificial intelligence (AI) innovations has led to a rise in AI harms—harms emerging from AI operations that pose significant threats to civil rights and democratic values in today’s technological landscape. A facial recognition system for improving criminal detection wrongly collected sensitive personal data and flagged racial minorities as shoplifters. A risk prediction algorithm adopted to identify patients denied medical treatment to Black individuals with poor health conditions. A social media algorithm intended to boost social engagement exacerbated addictive behavior and mental illness in teenagers. These harms are becoming increasingly ubiquitous. Yet, they often manifest in small and invisible forms, enabling them to aggregate while eluding regulatory oversight. Secretly and cumulatively, AI harms affect millions to billions of individuals. This Article constructs a legal typology to categorize these harms. It argues that there are four primary types of AI harms: eroding privacy, undermining autonomy, diminishing equality, and impairing safety. Additionally, it identifies two aggravating factors—accountability paucity and algorithmic opacity—that cause these seemingly minor harms to escalate into significant problems by obstructing harm detection and correction. This Article then conducts case studies of relevant legal frameworks in the United States, the European Union, and Japan to assess the effectiveness of existing responses to AI harms. The case studies reveal that these regulatory examples are insufficient; they either overlook certain types of harms or fail to consider their cumulative effects, thereby allowing problematic AI practices to circumvent legal obligations. Drawing on these findings, this Article proposes three legal interventions to address AI harms: each aims to mitigate primary harms by targeting * Assistant Professor of Law, American University Washington College of Law. I am grateful to Evan Caminker, Daniel Deacon, Rebecca Eisenberg, Andrew Guthrie Ferguson, Alex Klass, Aaron Perzanowski, J.J. Prescott, Nicholson Price, Steven Schaus, Paul Schwartz, Lawrence Solum, and Rebecca Wexler for generous feedback on earlier drafts. Thank you to participants at the Michigan Law Junior Scholars Forum, the Yale Law School Asian Law Junior Scholars Workshop, the Michigan Law Legal Theory Workshop, the Intellectual Property Scholars Conference at Berkeley Law, the Privacy Law Scholars Conference at Georgetown Law, the Michigan Junior Scholars Conference, the University of Michigan Ross Business School Symposium on Generative AI Law, Regulation, and Innovation in the Field, the Chengchi University Digital Governance, Law, and Policy Forum, the University of Michigan, Department of Molecular Cell and Developmental Biology, and the Hong Kong University Enhancing Personal Data Protection in the Age of Artificial Intelligence Conference. For thoughtful discussions, thanks to students in Professor Paul Schwartz’s Topics in Privacy and Security Law Seminar at Berkeley Law, Professor Lawrence Solum’s Course on Law and Artificial Intelligence at Virginia Law, Professor Anna Fisher’s Digital Culture Course at Michigan Digital Studies Institute, Professor Nicholson Price’s Artificial Intelligence and Law Seminar at Michigan Law, as well as my students in the Artificial Intelligence Regulation Seminar at Michigan Law and in Torts at AUWCL. ### Infrastructure & databases

Host, persistence layers.

- [Hetzner](entities/infrastructure/hetzner.md) — CPU-only VPS hosting Cemini (7 GB RAM, 41 Docker containers) — infrastructure, hosting, cemini-deployment
- [Cemini Stack — Two-Server Topology](entities/infrastructure/cemini-stack.md) — `[DRAFT 2026-05-03]` Umbrella entity for Cemini's two-server production deployment (CPX31 prod + CPX21 librarian, both Hetzner). Component map across the 41-container production stack + librarian (kb-server + stash + Ollama). Live-probed specs as of 2026-05-03 (prod 90% disk full ⚠). — infrastructure, cemini-deployment, two-server, hetzner
- [cemini-egress-fi — Finnish order-routing relay](entities/infrastructure/cemini-egress-fi.md) — `[DRAFT 2026-05-20]` The third Hetzner server (Helsinki, Finland) — egress relay routing Polymarket + Hyperliquid orders from a non-US IP. Near-idle (2 vCPU / 4 GB / 75 GB, load 0.00, sshd only); candidate host for the internal-phase Cemini AI assistant. Replaces the decommissioned Falkenstein predecessor (`cemini-poly-proxy`) — Finland covers both PM and HL whereas Germany only covered PM. — infrastructure, hetzner, order-routing, polymarket, hyperliquid, finland
- [DeepSeek Cloud API](entities/infrastructure/deepseek-api.md) — `[validated 2026-04-24 partial]` Cemini's LLM brain via DeepSeek's hosted Chat Completions API. Client layer confirmed (`services/llm_analyst/deepseek_client.py` called from linear 4-analyst StateGraph); scatter-gather topology + wiki_search RAG wiring pending Track B — infrastructure, llm, cloud-api, cemini-brain
- [Sakana Fugu (closed beta)](entities/infrastructure/sakana-fugu.md) — Multi-agent orchestration API (closed beta launched 2026-04-24, OpenAI-compatible endpoint). Productization of the Conductor + Trinity ICLR 2026 papers. Two-tier lineup (Mini 🐟 latency-optimized / Ultra 🐡 full orchestration); reported to beat Gemini 3.1 / GPT 5.4 / Opus 4.6 on GPQAD/LCBv6/SWEPro. Adds Option C to the scatter-gather P1 ticket — infrastructure, llm-orchestration, hosted-api, beta-product
- [LocalAI (mudler/LocalAI)](entities/infrastructure/localai.md) — Drop-in OpenAI-compatible local-inference shim; llama.cpp + GGUF backend, CPU-only optimized (MIT-licensed). K8 promotion 2026-04-28. Sits in the same architectural slot as the retracted DeepSeek-R1-14B local-runtime path; sibling to deepseek-api / ollama. Standardized API Shim architecture pattern — zero Python orchestration changes to swap `base_url=...` from cloud to local. Forward-looking IP-sale-prep entry; not deployed (no `localai` container in the 40-container Compose stack); 7 GB RAM constraint amplifies the GGUF-quantization story — infrastructure, llm-inference, local-models, openai-compatible-api, mit-licensed, k8-promotion
- [Ollama](entities/infrastructure/ollama.md) — `[RETRACTED 2026-04-24]` local-LLM runtime; not deployed (no hardware). See page for retraction record; see `deepseek-api.md` for the operative alternative.
- [DeepSeek-R1-14B](entities/infrastructure/deepseek-r1-14b.md) — `[RETRACTED 2026-04-24]` local-weights path; not deployed (no hardware). See page for retraction record; see `deepseek-api.md` for the model family via cloud API instead.

### Frameworks & libraries

Orchestration, web, validation, trading, RL.

- [LangGraph](entities/frameworks/langgraph.md) — Agent orchestration (Pregel BSP + Send API); scatter-gather topology — framework, llm-orchestration
- [FastAPI](entities/frameworks/fastapi.md) — Control-plane web framework; OpenAPI-generated — framework, web
- [FastMCP](entities/frameworks/fastmcp.md) — MCP server framework; decouples Cemini tools from any single LLM — framework, mcp
- [Pydantic v2](entities/frameworks/pydantic-v2.md) — Typed boundary at every seam; audit-safe serialization — library, validation
- [CCXT](entities/frameworks/ccxt.md) — Crypto exchange normalization layer — library, crypto, exchange-connectivity
- [NautilusTrader](entities/frameworks/nautilustrader.md) — Reference backtest + live-trading harness — framework, trading, backtest
- [TorchRL](entities/frameworks/torchrl.md) — PyTorch RL library; MAPPO training on Hetzner CPU — framework, reinforcement-learning
- [PettingZoo](entities/frameworks/pettingzoo.md) — Multi-agent RL environments (custom multi-venue exec env) — framework, multi-agent
- [FinBERT](entities/frameworks/finbert.md) — Finance-tuned BERT for sentiment; vendored locally — model, nlp, sentiment
- [AutoAgent (kevinrgu/autoagent)](entities/frameworks/autoagent.md) — Autonomous harness engineering framework (MIT-licensed). K8 promotion 2026-04-28. Two named patterns worth stealing: **Single-File Registry-Driven Harness** (consolidate the deployed 130 `@mcp.tool` decorators into one hot-reloadable file behind a fixed adapter boundary) + **Score-Driven Hill Climbing** (deterministic-score meta-evaluator commits-or-reverts harness modifications during offline windows). Forward-looking IP-sale-prep entry; not deployed — framework, agent-harness, score-driven-optimization, mit-licensed, k8-promotion
- [DSPy](entities/frameworks/dspy.md) — Wave 5.1 Adopt: Stanford prompt-programming framework (Apache-2.0). Declarative Signatures + GEPA optimization loops. Offline compilation pattern: optimize prompts on laptop → ship static strings to production LangGraph nodes. No DSPy runtime dependency on Hetzner. — framework, prompt-optimization, offline-compilation, deepseek-api, cemini-adopt

### Models

Pretrained and from-scratch ML models evaluated for Cemini integration.

- [Chronos-Bolt](entities/models/chronos-bolt.md) — K12 adoption: Amazon CPU-optimized time-series foundation model (Apache-2.0, T5-small ~8M params, ~80M series pretraining). Probabilistic quantile outputs. Fits Hetzner 7 GB CPU-only constraint. — model, time-series, foundation-model, cpu-inference, cemini-adopt
- [TimesFM](entities/models/timesfm.md) — Wave 5.1 Adopt + K239 code clone (7MB, checkpoints skipped). Google Research time-series foundation model (Apache-2.0, 200M params, 100B time-point pretraining, 16K context). Decoder-only with patch-tokenization; zero-shot outperforms ARIMA/ETS. ~400-800 MB RAM CPU-viable. Complementary to Chronos-Bolt (8M fast-path vs 200M deep-forecast). — model, time-series, foundation-model, cpu-inference, cemini-adopt, k239

Performance signals from TSAG tool-use benchmarks, training-free GRPO papers, MoE efficiency validations. Cemini's intended brain lineage. — concept, llm-models, deepseek, model-selection, cemini-brain

### AI safety & alignment

- [Latent Thought vs Chain of Thought — Computational Tradeoffs](concepts/latent-thought.md) — Latent thought (TCk, parallel) and CoT (approximate counting, sequential) enable different computational complexity classes — not a quality spectrum. Abstract CoT occupies the middle ground. Directly informs Conductor chat-vs-reasoner routing boundary. — concept, reasoning, llm-science, conductor-relevant
- [Policy Distillation — Multi-Expert Consolidation via RLVR and OPD](concepts/policy-distillation.md) — Three approaches on a continuum: token-priors (Training-Free GRPO, ~70% benefit), RLVR (reward-driven training), OPD (co-evolving student-teacher). Validates Conductor Path B → Path A sequence. — concept, rl-marl, ml-methods, grpo, conductor-relevant
- [Coding Agents as ML Engineers — AlphaZero Self-Play Pipeline](concepts/coding-agents-alphazero.md) — Frontier coding agents implement complete AlphaZero pipelines at solver-parity (Opus 4.7: 7/8 wins vs Pons). Jan→Apr 2026 saturation curve. GPT-5.4 sandbagging probe. Validates agent-as-ML-engineer for Conductor Path A + reusable benchmark template. — concept, llm-agents, coding-agents, conductor-relevant, validated
- [RecursiveMAS — System-Level Agentic Recursion](concepts/recursive-multi-agent-systems.md) — Latent-space agent collaboration via lightweight RecursiveLink modules; inner-outer loop co-optimization trains only 0.31% of parameters. Four collaboration patterns validated. Directly relevant to Conductor Path C (latent pipeline connectors replacing text handoffs). — concept, llm-agents, multi-agent-systems, latent-reasoning, system-optimization, conductor-relevant
- [Agent-Native Research Artifacts — Structured Knowledge over Narrative](concepts/agent-native-research-artifacts.md) — ARA protocol: four-layer agent-executable research package (logic/src/trace/evidence). Addresses Storytelling Tax (discarded failures) and Engineering Tax (underspecified implementation). Live Research Manager, ARA Compiler, and three-level Seal review protocol. Formalizes the structured-knowledge-over-narrative thesis this workspace already operates on. — concept, llm-agents, research-methodology, infrastructure, reproducibility, conductor-relevant
- [Exploration Graph & Dead-End Tracking — Preserving What Failed](concepts/exploration-graph-dead-ends.md) — Adopted from ARA: structured `## Dead Ends` wiki section (what was tried / why it failed / what was learned). Prevents re-discovering the same dead ends across sessions. Direct response to the 90.2% wasted-compute-on-failures finding. — concept, research-methodology, wiki-infrastructure, agent-memory
- [Evidence-Withheld Verification — Blind Judge Anti-Fabrication Pattern](concepts/evidence-withheld-verification.md) — Adopted from ARA Seal Level 3: judge receives task spec + candidate output, never the reference answer. Structural defense against fabrication-by-copying. Applied to ingest QA, Conductor routing evaluation, and claim verification. — concept, verification, prompt-design, agent-safety, anti-fabrication
- [Typed Relation Dependencies — imports / bounds / baseline](concepts/typed-relation-dependencies.md) — Adopted from ARA: optional `@I` (imports), `@B` (bounds), `@M` (baseline) prefixes on cross-links. Makes the wiki's relation graph machine-actionable without breaking existing untyped links. — concept, wiki-infrastructure, cross-linking, machine-actionable
- [Emergent Misalignment (EM)](concepts/emergent-misalignment.md) — Narrow finetuning → broad misaligned generalization across unrelated contexts (Betley et al., 2025b). Decomposes into unconditional + conditional components; three mitigations (data mixing, post-hoc HHH, inoculation prompting) suppress the former but gate the latter behind contextual triggers (Dubiński et al., 2026). Backdoor-like residual misalignment dormant under standard evals. — concept, ai-safety, alignment, emergent-misalignment
- [Nomotic AI — Pre-Action Authorization Architecture](concepts/nomotic-ai.md) — K12 adoption + K23 primary-source confirmation. Chris Hood (Jan 2026) introduces Nomotic AI as the governance counterpart to agentic AI. Six formal principles confirmed against primary paper. Cemini application: LangGraph brain → Trade Intent Payload → Pydantic v2 validation gate → broker API. Core principle: AI authority is delegated, never inherent; actions outside defined boundaries fail by default. — concept, ai-safety, agent-governance, pre-action-authorization, cemini-adopt
- [Algorithmic stopping conditions](concepts/algorithmic-stopping-conditions.md) — K12 adoption: deterministic O(1) debate termination. Router node enforces hard cutoff (max 3 rounds), confidence-delta check (<0.05 → early terminate), tool-gating (required tools must be called before agent output accepted). Guarantees bounded decision-loop complexity. — concept, ai-safety, agent-governance, debate-termination, cemini-adopt
- [Agent Authorization Stack — Five-Layer Architecture](concepts/agent-authorization-stack.md) — K19+K20+K22+K24+K25 cross-cluster synthesis. Five-layer authorization architecture: Nomotic AI (framework) → Reasoning Safety Monitor (detection) → SafeHarness (harness-internal monitoring) → SAE (enforcement) → ParlayMarket/RFQ trilemma (market-design constraints). Each layer independently verifiable; composition closes the gap from governance philosophy to market-structure constraint. IP-sale asset: demonstrates architectural depth beyond any single paper. — concept, authorization, governance, execution-safety, reasoning-safety, agent-security, cross-cluster-synthesis
- [LLM List-wise Alignment — Bayesian Ranking over Listwise Outputs](concepts/llm-listwise-alignment.md) — `[DRAFT 2026-05-07]` K33 ingest. Concept hub for BLADE methodology + 5 Cemini application surfaces (ticker watchlist re-ranking, news/source ranking, Conductor routing, brief screening, strategy ensemble selection). Diagnoses two failure modes (Indiscriminate Supervision + Gradient Decay) of BoN-style listwise rewards; specifies the Bayesian fix (prior over latent permutations × relevance-gap-scaled likelihood). — concept, ml-methods, listwise-alignment, ranking, conductor-relevant
- [Reviewer Agent — Inference-Time Feedback for Tool-Calling](concepts/reviewer-agent-inference-time-feedback.md) — `[DRAFT 2026-05-11]` K37 ingest from Apple Reinforced Agent paper. Reviewer-agent gates provisional tool calls pre-execution. Helpfulness-Harmfulness metric framework. Cemini integration: DeepSeek-R1 reviewer + DeepSeek-V3 base; selective-trigger gating on side-effecting tools (place_order, withdraw) to recover single-turn latency. — concept, agent-architecture, tool-calling, inference-time-feedback, conductor-relevant
- [Reviewer passive-injection corpus — K37 context bundle](concepts/reviewer-passive-injection-corpus.md) — `[VALIDATED 2026-07-08]` Manifest-driven `context_bundle.md` for K37 reviewer-agent; **XSP killer** (Robinhood lanes A/B) not Polymarket. Build: `scripts/build_reviewer_context.sh`. Prod wiring DEFERRED. — concept, conductor, reviewer-agent, passive-injection, xsp, k37
- [Agent Trajectory Triage — Cheap Non-LLM Signals for Sampling](concepts/agent-trajectory-triage-signals.md) — `[DRAFT 2026-05-11]` K37 ingest from DigitalOcean Signals paper. Signal taxonomy: interaction (misalignment/stagnation/disengagement/satisfaction), execution (failure/loop), environment (exhaustion). 82% vs 74% vs 54% informativeness. Cemini integration: Conductor audit JSONL signal-attachment, claude-mem observation prioritization, operator dashboard, future preference-data pipeline. ~200-300 LOC drop-in. — concept, agent-trajectories, triage, observability, signal-taxonomy, conductor-relevant
- [Verbalized Sampling — Training-Free Diversity Restoration for Aligned LLMs](concepts/verbalized-sampling-llm-diversity.md) — `[DRAFT 2026-05-11]` K39 ingest from Stanford/NEU/WVU VS paper. Mode collapse driver = typicality bias in preference data. Fix: prompt model for distribution-over-N-responses instead of single response. 1.6-2.1× diversity, +6.9 pts synthetic-SFT downstream accuracy, no factuality/safety loss. Third orthogonal training-free Conductor layer (after K37 reviewer-agent + signals). — concept, llm-prompting, diversity, mode-collapse, synthetic-data, training-free, conductor-relevant
- [RLHF Critic Bias — asymmetric-penalty preference-data failure mode and operational audit primitives](concepts/rlhf-critic-bias.md) — `[DRAFT 2026-05-11]` K40 ingest from Al-Zawahreh self-published April 2026 pre-print. **Sibling preference-data failure mode to VS**: where VS = typicality bias (annotators prefer familiar → mode collapse), Critic Bias = asymmetric-penalty (annotators penalize false endorsement > false criticism → systematic negativity on evals). Both data-level, both survive perfect reward models, both have training-free inference-time mitigations. Operational checklists (5 patterns + 6-cat Rhetorical Catalog + 5-tell Forensic Scorecard + 4 mitigations) port into `scripts/skill_audit.py`; polemical rhetoric (Ghost Committee, Patent Clerk Test) does not. Two-mode preference-data failure taxonomy now: typicality (VS) + asymmetric-penalty (Critic Bias) [+ sycophancy from Sharma 2024 = three-mode]. — concept, rlhf, llm-evaluation, preference-data, structural-bias, audit-primitives, conductor-relevant, methodology
- [TiDAR — Hybrid Diffusion-AR Language Model Architecture](concepts/tidar-hybrid-diffusion-ar.md) — `[DRAFT 2026-05-14]` K45 ingest from NVIDIA arXiv 2511.08923. Free-token-slot insight: unused sequence positions leveraged for parallel diffusion drafting. 3-section token partition (AR prefix / diffusion body / AR suffix) with structured attention masks. 4.71-5.91× throughput gains. Direct context-economics relevance: fewer tokens per generated symbol → cheaper Conductor sessions. — concept, llm-architecture, diffusion-models, throughput-optimization, k45
- [Counterfactual Prompting Baselines — Paraphrase-Null Distributions](concepts/counterfactual-prompting-baselines.md) — `[DRAFT 2026-05-14]` K45 ingest from arXiv 2605.01048. Statistical framework requiring paraphrase-null distributions for valid LLM counterfactual studies. Key empirical finding: 14.9% gender-swap flip rate statistically indistinguishable from 14.1% paraphrase baseline. Upgrades workspace [CONFIRMED] tag discipline — now implies paraphrase-null baseline check. — concept, llm-evaluation, counterfactual-prompting, causal-inference, statistical-methodology, k45

- [kb-server retrieval scaling — when (not) to add a vector index](concepts/kb-server-retrieval-scaling.md) — `[VALIDATED 2026-05-15]` K47 benchmark + decision. kb-server uses brute-force cosine over 943 vectors (one embedding/doc, no chunking); its own code says brute-force is fine to <2000 notes. Benchmarked turbovec (16µs/q, recall 0.92) and LEANN (19-min build, unstable search) vs brute-force (23µs/q, exact). **NO-GO** on wiring either into kb-server — premature at this scale + Node↔Python/Rust sidecar mismatch. Trigger to revisit: >2000 chunks → use `sqlite-vss` first. Both tools staged on cemini-librarian. — concept, kb-server, vector-search, benchmark, infrastructure, k47
- [Agent Memory Critique — Lookup vs True Memory](concepts/agent-memory-critique.md) — Current "agent memory" systems (vector stores, RAG, context-window) are lookup mechanisms (memos), not memory. Direct critique of kb-server/Stash architecture — legitimate memo use cases but not agent continuity. — concept, agent-memory, rag, infrastructure

- [Perps DEX Funding-Carry Strategy](concepts/perps-dex-funding-carry-strategy.md) — `[VALIDATED 2026-05-20]` K55 from @morpphhhaw. Cross-venue market-neutral funding-carry on perpetual-futures DEXes. Volatility-adjusted carry signal with stability + symmetry + rotation filters. 15-min allocator cycle. 8-gate safety architecture. Live test: ~96% of net PnL from funding, ~$0.06 net on 4 closed positions (~19% APR run-rate [TENTATIVE]). Out-of-scope-today / portable-failure-mode-catalog. — concept, perpetual-futures, funding-rate-arbitrage, market-neutral, cross-venue-hedging, k55
- [Sports-Prediction Consensus Agent](concepts/sports-prediction-consensus-agent.md) — `[VALIDATED 2026-05-20]` K55 from @stacyonchain. Multi-source consensus-as-signal Polymarket sports agent. Firecrawl tier-weighted scrape → Claude per-article signal extraction → Claude cross-source consensus → 3-gate entry (strength + 7pp edge + Kelly w/ 6% hard cap). Strong signal on ~15-20% of matches; consensus strength predicts better than direction. <$30/mo to run. CentPRO promotional layer is Reject; recipe is Adopt-candidate. — concept, polymarket, sports-trading, consensus-signal, firecrawl, claude-agent, k55
- [Time-Series ARIMA+GARCH Framework](concepts/time-series-arima-garch-framework.md) — `[VALIDATED 2026-05-20]` K55 from @RohOnChain. Standard quant time-series stack with operational discipline: unit-root → stationarity → ARMA family → GARCH volatility-clustering → walk-forward validation. **GARCH-based position sizing does more work than ARIMA forecast direction**. Designed to compose with Markov regime-switching (cross-validate on agreement/disagreement). — concept, quant-finance, time-series, arima, garch, walk-forward, k55
- [Multi-Module Bot Conflict-Detector Pattern](concepts/multi-module-bot-conflict-detector-pattern.md) — `[VALIDATED 2026-05-20]` K55 from @Gustafssonkotte. Two structural lessons: (1) **broken-data corruption** — phantom WINs + no-op gates + tier-field drops will silently sink a bot for weeks; source-of-truth = exchange exports. (2) **multi-module conflict-detector** — 3 modules (trend/levels/momentum) each output (p, strength); `spread > THRESHOLD → SKIP`. Removes false entries the system should never have been making. Time-aware sizing on short-resolution markets. 41% → 60% WR after rewrite [TENTATIVE single-day]. — concept, polymarket, bot-architecture, multi-signal-synthesis, conflict-detector, k55
- [Stochastic-Calculus Foundations](concepts/stochastic-calculus-foundations.md) — `[VALIDATED 2026-05-20]` K55 reference from Kakushadze "Phynance." Itô, Girsanov, martingales, Black-Scholes synthesis page. Backbone for resolving stochastic-calculus citations across the wiki. Path-integral framing. Polymarket-binary-pricing baseline note for any future tradeable-asset-binary markets. — concept, quant-finance, stochastic-calculus, foundations, k55
- [Hiring Quant Talent — Simons / Renaissance Philosophy](concepts/hiring-quant-talent-philosophy.md) — `[VALIDATED 2026-05-20]` K55 from the Simons JIC interview. Hire mathematicians + scientists, not finance professionals; slow thinkers > fast thinkers; one model continuously improved; data mining is fine; "we stick to the math." Templates several Cemini structural orientations. — concept, quant-finance, hiring, organizational-design, k55

#### K56 concept additions (2026-05-21)

- [Event-Based Prediction-Market ML](concepts/event-based-prediction-market-ml.md) — `[VALIDATED 2026-05-21]` K56 from @Alexo_0x. Five-part rejection-criteria framework for porting equity-style ML to Polymarket: per-event ADF stability filter, cross-event learning across 150-250 events OR synthetic Ornstein-Uhlenbeck pre-training, hard parameter budget `≤ samples/20` (hidden_size 8-16 normal, 64+ guarantees overfitting), temporal split by event `start_date` with per-event normalization, log-loss-vs-market kill rule over 5-7 events (90-day Sharpe doesn't work — too few events). Complements MCPT (different validation layer). — concept, polymarket, machine-learning, lstm, validation, event-based, k56
- [Polymarket Copy-Trading Strategy](concepts/polymarket-copy-trading-strategy.md) — `[DRAFT 2026-05-21]` K56 from @igor_mikerin + @DextersSolab. Four catastrophic mistakes: copying HFT/bot wallets (you buy their exit), short-track-record wallets (<3 months), viral wallets (saturation kills the edge), strategy-blind copying (**hedge-leg trap** = directional risk source never had). Wallet selection via polymarketanalytics.com filters. Pretracer + KreoPolyBot setup. Cemini relevance: adversary modeling + smart-wallet feed as alternative-data, not direct adoption. — concept, polymarket, copy-trading, smart-money, adversary-modeling, k56
- [Monte Carlo Permutation Test (MCPT)](concepts/monte-carlo-permutation-test-mcpt.md) — `[DRAFT 2026-05-21]` K56 from @phosphenq. Strategy-validation discipline per Timothy Masters 2020. Run strategy on N=1000+ permutations of price series (stationary block bootstrap, optimization included per permutation). p < 0.05 → real edge; otherwise data-mining bias. Goodhart applied to validation sets ("a measure that becomes a target is no longer a good measure"). Pre-deployment gate complementary to event-based ML caveats + regime-conditioned evaluation + live kill rules. — concept, quant-validation, backtest, monte-carlo, permutation-test, k56
- [Nested Learning (NL) — stub](concepts/nested-learning.md) — `[STUB 2026-05-13]` K43 osint-side cross-reference. Behrouz et al. - [Real-Assets Investing Framework (Massif)](concepts/real-assets-investing-framework.md) — `[DRAFT 2026-05-13]` K41 from Massif Capital Q4 2025 letter. ## How this index works

- **Sources** are grouped by first matching cluster (`rl-marl`, `llm-agents`, etc.). Multi-cluster tags are shown in `[+tag, +tag]` notation after the row.
- **Markers**: ⚠ = quality flag (review first-read); 🗂×N = N duplicate files folded into this canonical page.
- **Entities** are grouped by category (tickers, companies, people, facilities).
- **Concepts** are grouped by theme (ML/RL methodology, portfolio strategy, etc.) as enough accumulate.
- Scan headings → scan one-liners → open the 1–3 pages that look most relevant.
- When promoting a stub past `draft`: clean title + summary, add real cross-references, move the index row if the primary cluster changes.
